# PyMaCRUISE Porting Plan

## Port objective — owner clarification, 2026-10-14

Completion means reproducing the selected original Java/MATLAB BASE and PLUS behavior within the agreed comparison tolerances, including known limitations. Improving the original's anatomy, connectivity, nesting, or thickness is outside the current scope. The [validation policy](docs/validation-tolerances.md#port-acceptance-objective--owner-clarification-2026-10-14) distinguishes reference agreement from independent anatomical diagnostics and governs acceptance throughout this plan, including historical rows below. A matched original result can pass port acceptance even when both implementations fail a diagnostic quality target; absent reference evidence, equivalence remains unverified.

Prioritize matched-input stage and complete-workflow comparisons. Investigate anatomy flags when they help locate a Python-versus-original discrepancy. Preserve source behavior and stopping rules, including iteration caps; do not change algorithms merely to improve absolute quality metrics. Selected-stage parity does not close whole-workflow equivalence.

## Core validation strategy — owner direction, 2026-10-14

Use fresh runs of the supplied original Java and MATLAB implementations as the primary validation reference. Capture controlled BASE and PLUS runs with exact source/dependency versions, matched inputs/atlas/settings/geometry, intermediates, stopping conditions, and reproducible commands. Actual original routines and source wrappers are the reference; held-fixed Java comparisons remain stage evidence rather than a complete independent workflow.

Use the supplied precomputed outputs as secondary comparisons to define areas of concern. Historical settings, cached masks, transforms, and compiled refinement behavior are investigation leads, not reasons to postpone fresh source validation or optimize Python toward uncertain outputs. First establish Python-versus-fresh-original agreement; then explain differences between our original runs and the precomputed cases. See [the evidence hierarchy](docs/validation-tolerances.md#reference-evidence-hierarchy--owner-direction-2026-10-14).

Fresh MATLAB validation identified and corrected a Python refinement defect: numeric positive-integer `tabulate` rows were treated as distinct-values-only. Direct execution matches all 2,197 small-fixture voxels, all 6,092,928 voxels for each brain-resolution reference, and all 12,058,624 voxels of the refreshed human refinement. The historical downloaded human refinement remains different from fresh source. See [the fresh-source report](docs/reports/matlab-refinement-direct-source-comparison-2026-10-14.md) and [human BASE refresh report](docs/reports/in-vivo-base-refinement-refresh-2026-10-14.md).

## Progress and completion estimate

**Current status (2026-10-17):** The selected TOADS source-method estimate remains **3,511–4,911 of 13,651 lines (26–36%)**; this is a method-mapping estimate, not runtime coverage or workflow completion. The estimate is unchanged by this AtlasEM-only increment. Direct selected-class Java comparisons cover both AtlasEM optimizer routes on synthetic arrays: the post-recrop rigid optimizer (single scale) and initial three-level pyramid optimizer produce bit-identical six-parameter float32 transforms with nonzero movement. The pyramid trajectory is tested at iteration caps 1, 2, and 5, and a three-class synthetic case validates the selected-class registration mask. The full-image sampling correction from the single-scale comparison is retained. Focused direct-class regression: **6 passed in 7.95 s**. Full suite on implementation commit `f727c51431e19706433f262b8838f1be74d58fe4`: **1,652 passed, 4 skipped, 0 failed, 21 expected orientation warnings in 743.94 s**, peak sampled RSS **13.114 GB** under a 16 GB watchdog. See [single-scale comparison](docs/reports/atlasem-direct-demon-optimizer-2026-10-16.md), [pyramid comparison](docs/reports/atlasem-direct-demon-pyramid-optimizer-2026-10-17.md), [trajectory regression](docs/reports/atlasem-direct-demon-trajectory-2026-10-17.md), [class-selection comparison](docs/reports/atlasem-demon-selected-classes-2026-10-17.md), and [full-suite evidence](docs/reports/full-pytest-2026-10-17-atlasem-demon-selected-classes.md). Selected `ibsr8obj` assets remain unavailable, so selected-atlas initialization, human AtlasEM convergence, pre-LesionTOADS integration, and complete PLUS equivalence remain open. The CLI reference covers all 38 commands; TOADS line estimates are unchanged.

**Latest human BASE TGDM evidence (2026-10-16):** the regenerated attempt-02 exact crop passes selected Java surface-position gates on all three surfaces; outer Hausdorff is 0.710 mm. Full level-set values still differ, and this remains held-fixed stage evidence rather than whole-workflow equivalence; see [attempt-02 TGDM comparison](docs/reports/in-vivo-root-sample-base-attempt-02-tgdm-2026-10-16.md).

**Latest human PLUS source evidence (2026-10-16):** the selected Java LesionTOADS Dura-output method reproduces the saved CSF, GM, WM-fill, and WM-mask outputs exactly after wrapper scaling on the same 164×207×196 RAS crop. This is a held-fixed output-stage check; full PLUS source equivalence remains open. See [Dura-output comparison](docs/reports/in-vivo-100260-plus-dura-output-java-comparison-2026-10-16.md).

**PLUS source-stage coverage finding (2026-10-16):** the selected MATLAB wrapper runs AtlasEM and a Dura-removal pass before LesionTOADS. Those stages are absent from the current Python nine-stage PLUS workflow; the selected 8-object `ibsr8obj` AtlasEM descriptor/payload is not present in `data/atlases/` or the inspected source checkouts. Fixed-transform prior mapping, crop/uncrop, registration-result recropping, and registration-pyramid input preparation now have synthetic source-excerpt comparisons. The post-recrop full-resolution rigid stage now has a source-faithful single-scale adapter, while direct Java optimizer-output comparison, selected asset initialization, and full pre-LesionTOADS integration remain open. See the [source-stage audit](docs/reports/plus-source-stage-coverage-audit-2026-10-16.md) and P44b.

**Corrected source profile integrated on case 100260:** The V3 Dura pair is WM `0.05` / GM `0.02`; unmasked N4 uses rescaling off because the MATLAB wrapper supplies no mask. The fresh eight-stage no-regrid BASE run completed and resumed with 68/68 hashes verified, 51 finite RAS NIfTIs, and peak RSS 8.027 GB. Both TOADS passes stopped naturally and match selected Java exactly on matched inputs. The supplied historical output still fails refinement Dice and surface-distance tolerances, and historical N4 differs (MAE 6,165.91), so whole-workflow equivalence remains open. Focused N4/CLI/PLUS-preparation checks pass 8 tests. The full suite passes 1,577 tests, 4 skipped, 0 failed, with peak RSS 15.218 GB. See the [corrected N4 run report](docs/reports/in-vivo-base-v3-no-mask-n4-source-profile-2026-10-14.md), [N4 profile correction](docs/reports/n4-no-mask-rescaling-default-correction-2026-10-14.md), and [full-suite report](docs/reports/full-pytest-2026-10-14-n4-no-mask-profile.md).

For detailed row-by-row evidence, including full-suite reports and tested commit hashes for Done rows, use the acceptance table and linked reports below. These statuses are intentionally narrower than “equivalent to the full MATLAB/JIST pipeline.”

This is the step-by-step implementation and acceptance plan for a Python-native MaCRUISE. Work proceeds from source contracts and synthetic cases to stage-by-stage ports, and only then to a small, approved set of real reference cases. Synthetic image volumes, phantoms, and expected outputs created for this project are intended to be committed under `tests/data/synthetic/`; no human imaging data belongs in that directory.

## Completion definition

The port is complete when the base MaCRUISE workflow (T1 + MaSeg) and the selected MaCRUISEplus workflow (T1 + MaSeg/TOADS inputs + FLAIR, as defined by the source entry point) can be installed, configured, run, resumed, and produce documented volume and surface outputs without MATLAB or a MIPAV/JIST JVM runtime. Each stage must have automated checks. Synthetic cases must exercise the contracts and edge cases before reference MRI is introduced. Final equivalence must be assessed against reproducible MATLAB/JIST outputs from approved reference cases, with per-output tolerances, metrics, and any known deviations recorded in the repository.

Independent anatomical quality targets are diagnostic rather than completion gates. Human surfaces and thickness need agreement with matched original outputs, not improvement beyond the reference. Source-matched failures or capped stops are acceptable when documented; missing reference evidence remains an equivalence gap.

“Reasonable numerical error” is not one tolerance for every output: exact label/mask operations should be exact; floating-point image stages require measured, operation-specific tolerances; surfaces and thickness require geometric/statistical tolerances. Proposed criteria in the table are minimum gates. Establish and record the final per-stage tolerances against the reference implementation before claiming equivalence.

## Working rules

- Execute rows in dependency order. A row is **validated** only when its stated checks pass and evidence (test name/report, metrics, or source decision) is recorded. Do not fill a validation date before that evidence exists.
- For every **Done** row, record validation appropriate to its scope: focused tests and the closest affected integration tests for localized changes; a full-repository `pytest` run only at major workflow-integration milestones and final acceptance, or sooner when a change crosses multiple algorithm families or shared image/geometry contracts. Include the exact command, pass/fail/skip counts, duration, and tested commit in the row or its linked report. Existing full-suite reports remain valid historical evidence for their stated commits; do not rerun the multi-minute suite merely to close a localized row.
- Keep test data synthetic until the Synthetic Readiness Gate. That gate requires fixtures for geometry, label maps, tissue boundaries, bias fields, transforms, surfaces, and lesion cases, and automated tests that pass without downloading or mounting subject data.
- Commit deterministic synthetic input volumes and small expected-result fixtures. Generate large or derivable fixtures in tests when doing so is simpler and deterministic; commit the generator and its seed. Every fixture must have a description, dimensions, spacing/affine, value or label meaning, provenance (synthetic), and license note where relevant.
- Use the MATLAB/JIST wrappers as workflow/parameter contracts and VandyCRUISE as the selected algorithm reference. Reuse NiBabel, NumPy, SciPy, ANTsPy, and scikit-image for general operations where behavior matches. Select mesh I/O/geometry tools only as needed for scientific surface outputs; do not add rendering/viewer tools to the port. Do not substitute a generic algorithm for TOADS, topology correction, ACE, GVF, or CRUISE/TGDM without equivalence evidence.
- Do not require FreeSurfer to install, configure, run, validate, or use PyMaCRUISE. Do not require FreeSurfer data, licenses, or outputs; historical references in source notes are explanatory only.
- Store every image file managed by the project as compressed NIfTI (`.nii.gz`): bundled image assets, synthetic fixtures, persisted inputs, intermediates, and outputs. Normalize images supplied in other formats before storing or reusing them. Keep text/configuration/mesh formats appropriate to their non-image role.
- Express all spatial outputs in RAS: volume voxel axes use RAS orientation and surface/point coordinates use RAS+ millimetres. Reorient every non-RAS spatial input before use, display a warning, and record the input frame/orientation and conversion in the run manifest.
- Run real-data checks only after synthetic unit/stage gates pass. Use a small, de-identified, approved reference set; do not commit those images or outputs. Record access-controlled locations and hashes/identifiers only where policy permits.

## Step-by-step acceptance table

Validation evidence for completed rows is recorded in the row or a linked report under `docs/reports/`. Each entry names the row-specific focused and/or integration checks and the tested commit; major integration gates and final acceptance also cite the corresponding full-suite run. Historical rows may retain full-suite evidence from their original implementation commits.

Dates use `YYYY-MM-DD`. `—` means not started/not yet validated. Status values: **Done** (documentation/planning artifact complete), **Started**, **Blocked**, and **Not started**. “Done” on the plan row means the plan exists; it does not imply implementation or validation.

## Real-data gate and data handling

Do not use actual imaging data to fill gaps in ordinary development when the same contract can be tested synthetically. P35 is the required readiness review. Actual MRI is reserved for checking biological realism, whole-workflow interactions, and equivalence to the historical pipeline; it cannot replace deterministic synthetic unit/stage tests. Real cases and derived images stay outside Git unless the user explicitly approves an appropriate, shareable dataset and all applicable data-use requirements are satisfied.

## Progress recording

When work begins on a row, set status to **Started** and enter its start date. When its validation criteria pass, record the evidence briefly in the relevant source/test/report, set status to **Done**, and enter the validation date. If work is blocked by missing source/assets/behavior, name the blocker in the row or the decision log rather than marking the step complete. Keep `readme.md` as a short entry point and current summary; this file is the authoritative detailed roadmap.

Retain signed `nIterMax` behavior and `narrow_band_competition` growth suppression.
